CASA: Classification Augmented with Safety Attention for Robust Multimodal Alignment
Authors: Anurag Kumar, Raghuveer Peri, Jon Burnsky, Alexandru Nelus, Rohit Paturi, Srikanth Vishnubhotla, Yanjun Qi
First: 2026-03-31T23:19:50+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-06T22:04:31+00:00
Comments: 9 pages + Appendix section
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multimodal large-language models (MLLMs) often experience degraded safety alignment when harmful queries exploit cross-modal interactions. Models aligned on text alone show a higher rate of successful attacks when extended to two or more modalities. We propose a simple conditional decoding strategy, CASA (Classification Augmented with Safety Attention) that uses internal representations of MLLMs to predict a binary safety token before response generation. The classification is guided by a safety attention mechanism that computes attention between a dedicated query prompt and the input to scale the classification logits, sharpening the model's ability to detect malicious queries. Our design ensures robust safety alignment without relying on any external classifier or auxiliary head, and without modality-specific safety fine-tuning. Unlike prior safety-decoding methods that attach an external classifier or moderation head, or rescale every generated token with a reward model, CASA makes a single, internal, attention-gated safety decision over the shared multimodal representation, which is precisely what lets safety learned from text-only training transfer to image and audio inputs. Compared against 4 baseline models across 7 attack types spanning text, image, and audio modalities, evaluated on benchmarks such as MM-SafetyBench, JailbreakV-28k, and adversarial audio tests, CASA lowers the average attack success rate by >90% relative when paired with a standard safety prompt (applied to all baselines for fair comparison), and by 54--61% using its internal safety mechanism alone. Our empirical evaluations also show that CASA~maintains strong utility on benign inputs, validated through both automated and human evaluations (via 13 trained annotators). Together, these results highlight CASA as a simple and generalizable framework to improve multimodal LLM safety.
中文标题/摘要
标题：CASA：增强安全注意力的分类用于稳健的多模态对齐
多模态大语言模型（MLLMs）在有害查询利用跨模态交互时，往往会出现安全对齐下降的问题。仅基于文本对齐的模型在扩展到两个或更多模态时，成功攻击的比率更高。我们提出了一种简单的条件解码策略CASA（增强安全注意力的分类），该策略利用MLLMs的内部表示在生成响应之前预测二元安全标记。分类由安全注意力机制引导，该机制计算专用查询提示与输入之间的注意力，以缩放分类logits，增强模型检测恶意查询的能力。我们的设计确保了稳健的安全对齐，而不依赖于任何外部分类器或辅助头，也不需要特定模态的安全微调。与之前的安全解码方法不同，CASA在共享的多模态表示上做出单一的内部注意力门控安全决策，这正是使得从仅文本训练中学到的安全性能够转移到图像和音频输入的原因。与4个基线模型在7种攻击类型（涵盖文本、图像和音频模态）进行比较，并在MM-SafetyBench、JailbreakV-28k和对抗音频测试等基准上进行评估，CASA在与标准安全提示（对所有基线进行公平比较）配对时，平均攻击成功率降低了>90%，仅使用其内部安全机制时降低了54%至61%。我们的实证评估还表明，CASA在良性输入上保持强大的实用性，通过13名训练过的注释员进行的自动和人工评估得到了验证。这些结果共同突显了CASA作为一个简单且可推广的框架，以提高多模态LLM的安全性。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the issue of degraded safety alignment in multimodal large-language models (MLLMs), which are vulnerable to harmful queries that exploit cross-modal interactions. Previous methods often relied on external classifiers or moderation heads, leading to limitations in robustness and transferability of safety measures across modalities. The proposed CASA (Classification Augmented with Safety Attention) approach introduces a conditional decoding strategy that utilizes internal representations of MLLMs to predict a binary safety token before generating responses, guided by a safety attention mechanism. This method enhances the model's ability to detect malicious queries without the need for external classifiers or modality-specific fine-tuning. The paper demonstrates that CASA significantly reduces the average attack success rate by over 90% compared to baseline models across various attack types and maintains strong performance on benign inputs, establishing it as a robust framework for improving multimodal LLM safety.
本研究解决了多模态大语言模型（MLLMs）在安全对齐方面的挑战，这些模型在有害查询利用跨模态交互时往往存在脆弱性。以往的方法通常依赖外部分类器或审核头，导致在鲁棒性和跨模态安全措施的可转移性方面存在局限性。提出的CASA（分类增强安全注意力）方法引入了一种条件解码策略，利用MLLMs的内部表示来预测二元安全标记，借助安全注意力机制增强模型检测恶意查询的能力，而无需外部组件。该方法显著改善了安全对齐，并经过实证验证，在各种攻击类型和模态中实现了超过90%的平均攻击成功率降低，同时在良性输入上保持强劲性能，从而证明了其有效性和增强多模态LLM安全性的普遍适用性。
Item Response Theory for AI Safety
Authors: Joshua Fonseca Rivera, Neil Shah, David Demitri Africa, Konstantinos Voudouris
First: 2026-08-05T17:25:27+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-05T17:25:27+00:00
Comments: 15 pages, 9 figures, 6 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Language models differ in how safely they behave and these differences are measured by safety benchmarks. But aggregated benchmark scores are hard to trust and interpret, because benchmarks duplicate one another, correlate heavily, and models may sandbag when they detect evaluation. To address these issues, we draw on Item Response Theory (IRT), a statistical toolkit for measuring these latents from performance on items with inferred psychometric properties. We fit IRT models to eight safety benchmarks across 192 language models, the largest psychometric analysis of LLM safety evaluations to date, and contribute three results. First, we find that three interpretable factors of refusal strictness, truthfulness, and contextual harm explain most of the variance between models across benchmarks. Second, psychometrically selected items recover full benchmark scores with lower error than random subsets of the same size, and roughly ten adaptively chosen items suffice for several individual benchmarks, cutting evaluation cost by 97-99%. Third, IRT supports audits of individual models, showing that it can be used to detect naive sandbagging and changes of model behind APIs. Overall, we show IRT is a ready-made toolkit for reading, reducing, and auditing safety benchmarks, which we recommend frontier labs and evaluators adopt.
中文标题/摘要
标题：人工智能安全的项目反应理论
语言模型在安全性表现上存在差异，这些差异通过安全基准进行测量。然而，聚合的基准分数难以信任和解释，因为基准之间存在重复、强相关，并且模型在检测到评估时可能会故意降低表现。为了解决这些问题，我们借鉴了项目反应理论（IRT），这是一种通过对具有推断心理测量特性的项目表现进行测量的统计工具包。我们将IRT模型拟合到192个语言模型的八个安全基准上，这是迄今为止对大型语言模型安全评估的最大心理测量分析，并贡献了三个结果。首先，我们发现拒绝严格性、真实性和上下文伤害这三个可解释因素解释了模型在基准之间的大部分方差。其次，心理测量选择的项目以低于随机子集的误差恢复完整的基准分数，且大约十个自适应选择的项目足以满足几个单独的基准，将评估成本降低了97-99%。第三，IRT支持对单个模型的审计，表明它可以用于检测幼稚的故意降低表现和API背后模型的变化。总体而言，我们展示了IRT是一个现成的工具包，用于解读、减少和审计安全基准，我们建议前沿实验室和评估者采用。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of evaluating the safety of language models, which is complicated by the unreliability of aggregated benchmark scores due to their redundancy and correlation, as well as the potential for models to manipulate evaluations. Previous methods have struggled with these issues, leading to a lack of trust in safety assessments. The proposed approach utilizes Item Response Theory (IRT) to analyze safety benchmarks more effectively, providing a clearer understanding of model performance and reducing evaluation costs significantly. This paper contributes by fitting IRT models to eight safety benchmarks across 192 language models, revealing three key factors that explain variance in model behavior and demonstrating that fewer, carefully selected items can yield more reliable results. The methodology shows that IRT can effectively audit models and detect evaluation manipulation, achieving a substantial reduction in evaluation costs while maintaining performance integrity.
本研究解决了测量语言模型安全性的问题，这些模型的行为差异难以解释，因为现有安全基准之间存在冗余和相关性。以往的方法在这些问题上表现不佳，导致聚合得分不可靠，并可能在评估过程中出现模型操控。提出的方法利用项目反应理论（IRT）分析安全基准，通过识别可解释的因素，如拒绝严格性、真实性和上下文危害，提供了对模型性能的更细致理解。这种方法论允许以显著降低的成本进行更准确的评估，因为研究表明，少量精心选择的项目可以有效替代更大基准集。研究结果表明，IRT可以增强安全评估的可靠性，并促进对单个模型的审计，从而支持改善人工智能安全评估的目标。
Robust General Utility for Reinforcement Learning
Authors: Zixuan Liu, Fangzheng Wu, Brian Summa, Zizhan Zheng
First: 2026-08-04T12:28:39+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-04T12:28:39+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Reinforcement learning (RL) with general utility extends classic RL by optimizing an arbitrary utility functional of the policy-induced occupancy measure, thereby enabling a broader range of applications. However, previous work on general utility RL typically assumes the evaluation utility is fixed and correctly specified. In practice, the utility used at deployment can deviate from the training one, creating a robustness gap that prior work does not address. Motivated by this, we propose robust general-utility RL, a minimax learning framework that trains policies against utility misspecification within a prescribed uncertainty set. Our framework strictly generalizes standard general-utility RL while also providing a unified view of many existing RL frameworks, including reward-robust RL and constrained RL, through appropriate choices of the utility uncertainty set. We further develop provably convergent stochastic algorithms for two regimes. For concave utilities, we develop a projected stochastic gradient descent-ascent method and establish stationarity guarantees. For the more challenging nonconcave regime, we propose a stochastic prox-extragradient algorithm that mitigates ill-posed behavior induced by nonconcavity, with convergence guarantees to approximate first-order stationarity. Experiments on LLM safety alignment and exploration maximization tasks further corroborate the convergence behavior consistent with our theory.
中文标题/摘要
标题：强化学习的鲁棒一般效用
具有一般效用的强化学习（RL）通过优化策略引起的占用测度的任意效用泛函，扩展了经典RL，从而使其能够应用于更广泛的场景。然而，以往关于一般效用RL的研究通常假设评估效用是固定且正确指定的。在实践中，部署时使用的效用可能与训练时的效用有所偏离，造成了以往研究未能解决的鲁棒性差距。基于此，我们提出了鲁棒一般效用RL，这是一种最小最大学习框架，旨在针对规定的不确定性集内的效用误指定训练策略。我们的框架严格推广了标准的一般效用RL，同时通过适当选择效用不确定性集，提供了许多现有RL框架（包括奖励鲁棒RL和约束RL）的统一视角。我们进一步为两种情况开发了可证明收敛的随机算法。对于凹效用，我们开发了一种投影随机梯度下降-上升方法，并建立了平稳性保证。对于更具挑战性的非凹情况，我们提出了一种随机近似外梯度算法，减轻了非凹性引起的不良行为，并保证收敛以近似一阶平稳性。关于LLM安全对齐和探索最大化任务的实验进一步证实了与我们理论一致的收敛行为。
Summary / 总结
This paper addresses the limitations of traditional reinforcement learning (RL) methods that optimize a fixed utility functional, which often leads to a robustness gap when the utility at deployment differs from that used during training. The proposed robust general-utility RL framework introduces a minimax learning approach that accounts for utility misspecification within a defined uncertainty set, thereby generalizing standard general-utility RL and integrating various existing RL frameworks. The contribution lies in the development of stochastic algorithms that ensure convergence for both concave and nonconcave utility functions, with specific methods tailored to each regime. Experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach in tasks related to LLM safety alignment and exploration maximization, supporting the framework's robustness and theoretical guarantees.
本文解决了传统强化学习（RL）方法的局限性，这些方法优化固定的效用函数，当部署时的效用与训练时不同，可能导致鲁棒性差距。所提出的鲁棒一般效用RL引入了一种极小极大学习框架，通过在定义的不确定性集合内训练策略来考虑效用误指定，从而推广现有的RL框架并解决鲁棒性问题。贡献在于为凹和非凹效用场景开发了随机算法，确保收敛到一阶平稳性。实验结果表明，所提出的方法在LLM安全对齐和探索最大化任务中有效，支持了在RL应用中鲁棒性和适应性的目标。
EduZone: A Framework for Evaluating LLM Safety for K-12 Students and Teachers
Authors: Junyeong Park, Jieun Han, Haneul Yoo, So-Yeon Ahn, Jinsung Yoon, Alice Oh
First: 2026-08-03T10:23:42+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-03T10:23:42+00:00
Comments: Under Review
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used across diverse tasks in K-12 education, yet existing safety evaluations rarely examine how harmful or inappropriate content appears in interactions between LLMs and students or teachers. To address this, we present EduZone, an evaluation framework for LLM safety across diverse educational scenarios. Our framework systematically combines (1) student- and teacher-facing LLM usage contexts, (2) fine-grained curriculum concepts, and (3) 6 risk categories and 28 subcategories spanning both conventional and education-specific harms to generate contextually grounded adversarial interactions. We construct these interactions in three settings: single-turn requests, static multi-turn conversations, and dynamic multi-turn conversations. Using these interactions, we evaluate ten LLMs using four safety levels: refusal, safe assistance, risky assistance with safety guidance, and fully risky assistance. Our results reveal greater vulnerability to education-specific risks and dynamic multi-turn interactions, while existing safety guardrails fail to adequately address these risks. EduZone advances LLM safety in education by providing an automated, scalable evaluation framework that supports the development and deployment of safer LLMs in K-12 education.
中文标题/摘要
标题：EduZone：评估K-12学生和教师LLM安全性的框架
大型语言模型（LLMs）在K-12教育中越来越多地用于各种任务，但现有的安全评估很少检查LLMs与学生或教师之间的互动中出现的有害或不当内容。为了解决这个问题，我们提出了EduZone，一个针对不同教育场景的LLM安全评估框架。我们的框架系统地结合了（1）面向学生和教师的LLM使用背景，（2）细化的课程概念，以及（3）涵盖传统和教育特定危害的6个风险类别和28个子类别，以生成具有情境基础的对抗性互动。我们在三种设置中构建这些互动：单轮请求、静态多轮对话和动态多轮对话。利用这些互动，我们使用四个安全级别评估十个LLM：拒绝、安全协助、带安全指导的风险协助和完全风险协助。我们的结果揭示了对教育特定风险和动态多轮互动的更大脆弱性，而现有的安全防护措施未能充分应对这些风险。EduZone通过提供一个自动化、可扩展的评估框架，推动了教育中LLM的安全性，支持更安全的LLM在K-12教育中的开发和部署。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the increasing use of large language models (LLMs) in K-12 education and the inadequacy of existing safety evaluations that overlook harmful content in interactions between LLMs and students or teachers. Previous methods have failed to comprehensively assess the risks associated with educational contexts, particularly in dynamic multi-turn conversations. The proposed EduZone framework differs by systematically integrating various LLM usage contexts, curriculum concepts, and a detailed categorization of risks to create contextually relevant adversarial interactions. This approach is well-motivated as it aims to enhance the safety of LLMs in educational settings. The methodology involves evaluating ten LLMs across four safety levels using interactions constructed in three different conversational settings. The findings indicate that LLMs are particularly vulnerable to education-specific risks, highlighting the limitations of existing safety measures and demonstrating that EduZone effectively supports the development of safer LLMs for K-12 education.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLMs）在K-12教育中的日益使用，以及现有安全评估未能考虑与学生和教师互动中有害内容的不足。以往的方法未能充分检查教育特定风险，导致对LLMs在教育环境中可能造成威胁的理解存在空白。提出的EduZone框架通过系统整合各种使用场景、课程概念和风险类别，创建上下文相关的对抗性互动，从而有所不同。这一动机充分的方法通过提供一种自动化和可扩展的评估方法，为LLM安全性做出了贡献。该方法论涉及在不同互动类型和安全级别下评估十个LLMs，揭示了教育特定场景中的显著脆弱性，并表明当前的安全措施不足，从而支持了在K-12教育中改进安全协议的必要性。
Single Canonical Prompts Underestimate LLM Safety's Surface-Form Sensitivity
Authors: Yongxi Zhou, Junwei Yao, Yuanzhe Liu, Zihan Dong, Wenbo Ye, Jiaxi Wen, Lai Yun Choi
First: 2026-08-01T22:28:31+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-01T22:28:31+00:00
Comments: 9 pages, 3 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
A benchmark score is a measurement instrument, yet most benchmarks read each item at a single canonical surface form. We ask whether that reading is faithful: when an item's intent is held fixed and only its meaning-preserving surface form varies, does the canonical-form score estimate model behavior well, and how much of any variation is decoding/judge noise rather than signal? We instantiate this in safety, a high-stakes setting with no gold label to average toward. To avoid prior confounds, we pre-author the reformulations (refusal-free, mostly non-LLM: machine back-translation and a Matrix-Language-Frame code-switch generator) so an identical surface form reaches every model, score all responses with one human-anchored, vendor-neutral judge (Claude, kappa = 0.86 vs. human on unsafe compliance, stable across languages, cross-checked by GPT-4o), and verify intent preservation. On 370 seeds x 5 surface forms x 5 models, no single transformation is uniformly most dangerous (6 of 20 per-transformation McNemar tests survive correction, most protective). Yet evaluating only the canonical prompt underestimates unsafe compliance: the union of unsafe outcomes across forms exceeds even the worst single form by 3.3-12.9 pp, with bootstrap 95% CIs excluding zero for all five models, and 5-13% of seeds safe on canonical are unsafe under some reformulation -- above a zero stochasticity floor (canonical resampled five times at temperature 0 gives 0/370 new exposures). The size of this gap is model-dependent (largest on Gemini 2.5 Pro). One form recovers only ~53% of a model's observed unsafe surface and about three reach 85% -- a redundancy characterization of this form set, not of a defined population. A benign control (XSTest) suggests the instability is bidirectional, though the benign and harmful pools are not item-matched. We release the dataset, code, and per-response labels.
中文标题/摘要
标题：单一规范提示低估了大型语言模型安全性的表面形式敏感性
基准分数是一种测量工具，但大多数基准以单一规范表面形式读取每个项目。我们询问这种读取是否忠实：当一个项目的意图保持不变，仅其保持意义的表面形式变化时，规范形式分数是否能很好地估计模型行为，以及任何变化中有多少是解码/评判噪声而非信号？我们在安全性这一高风险环境中实例化这一点，因其没有金标准可供平均。为了避免先前的混淆，我们预先授权了重构（无拒绝，主要非大型语言模型：机器回译和矩阵语言框架代码切换生成器），以便相同的表面形式到达每个模型，所有响应由一个人类锚定的、中立的评判者（Claude，kappa = 0.86与人类在不安全合规性上的比较，在各语言间稳定，由GPT-4o交叉验证）进行评分，并验证意图保持。在370个种子x 5个表面形式x 5个模型中，没有单一变换是普遍最危险的（20个每变换的McNemar测试中有6个在校正后存活，大多数是保护性的）。然而，仅评估规范提示低估了不安全合规性：跨形式的不安全结果的并集甚至超过了最糟糕的单一形式3.3-12.9个百分点，所有五个模型的自助法95%置信区间均不包括零，且5-13%的在规范下安全的种子在某些重构下是不安全的——高于零随机性底线（规范在温度0下重采样五次给出0/370个新暴露）。这一差距的大小依赖于模型（在Gemini 2.5 Pro上最大）。一种形式仅恢复了模型观察到的不安全表面的约53%，而大约三种形式达到了85%——这是对这一形式集的冗余特征，而非定义人口的特征。一个良性对照（XSTest）表明不稳定性是双向的，尽管良性和有害池并未按项目匹配。我们发布了数据集、代码和每个响应标签。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of existing benchmark scores in evaluating large language models (LLMs) by questioning the effectiveness of using a single canonical surface form to assess model behavior, particularly in safety contexts where no gold standard exists. Previous methods often overlooked the variability in model responses due to different surface forms, leading to potential misestimations of unsafe compliance. The proposed approach involves pre-authoring multiple reformulations of prompts and using a consistent human-anchored judge to evaluate responses across various models, thus ensuring intent preservation and reducing noise in the scoring process. The study's methodology includes testing 370 seeds with five surface forms across five models, revealing that relying solely on canonical prompts significantly underestimates unsafe outcomes, with the union of unsafe results across forms exceeding the worst single form by 3.3-12.9 percentage points. This highlights the model-dependent nature of safety assessments and contributes a dataset and code for further research in LLM safety evaluation.
本研究探讨了现有基准评估在评估大型语言模型（LLMs）安全性方面的局限性，这些评估通常依赖单一的规范表面形式进行评分。以往的方法未能充分捕捉到模型行为在表面形式变化时的变异性，导致对不安全合规性的低估。所提出的方法涉及预先编写多个提示的改写，以确保在变化表面形式的同时保持意图一致，并使用人类锚定的评审进行评分，以减少噪声。研究方法包括在五种表面形式和五个模型上测试370个种子，结果显示，仅评估规范提示显著低估了不安全结果，跨表面形式的不安全结果总和超过最差单一形式3.3-12.9个百分点。这表明所提出的方法有效地突出了LLM安全性对表面形式变化的敏感性，为未来研究提供了有价值的见解和数据集。
Quality-Diversity Red-Teaming: Automated Generation of High-Quality and Diverse Attackers for Large Language Models
Authors: Ren-Jian Wang, Ke Xue, Zeyu Qin, Ziniu Li, Sheng Tang, Hao-Tian Li, Shengcai Liu, Zhi Yu, Yuanpeng Tan, Chao Qian
First: 2025-06-08T13:07:41+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-01T10:21:24+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Ensuring the safety and robustness of large language models (LLMs) is a fundamental challenge and a critical prerequisite for the responsible deployment of artificial intelligence. Red-teaming, a systematic framework to identify adversarial prompts that elicit harmful responses from target LLMs, has emerged as a crucial safety evaluation paradigm. Within this framework, the diversity of adversarial prompts is critical for a comprehensive safety assessment. However, previous red-teaming approaches often pursue diversity through simplistic metrics such as word frequency or sentence embedding similarity, which may not capture meaningful variation in attack strategies. In addition, the common practice of training a single attacker model restricts coverage across all potential attack styles and risk categories. This paper introduces Quality-Diversity Red-Teaming (QDRT), a new framework designed to address these limitations. QDRT achieves goal-driven diversity through behavior-conditioned training and implements a behavioral replay buffer in an open-ended manner. In addition, it trains multiple specialized attackers capable of generating high-quality attacks across diverse styles and risk categories. Our empirical evaluation demonstrates that QDRT generates attacks that are both more diverse and more effective against a wide range of target LLMs, including open-source models \texttt{GPT-2}, \texttt{Llama-3}, \texttt{Gemma-2}, \texttt{Qwen2.5}, and commercial models \texttt{GPT-4.1} and \texttt{GPT-5-Chat}. This work advances the field of LLM safety by providing a systematic and effective approach to automated red-teaming, ultimately supporting the responsible deployment of LLMs. Our code is available at https://github.com/lamda-bbo/QDRT.
中文标题/摘要
标题：质量-多样性红队：大型语言模型高质量和多样化攻击者的自动生成
确保大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全性和鲁棒性是一个基本挑战，也是负责任地部署人工智能的关键前提。红队是一种系统框架，用于识别引发目标LLMs有害响应的对抗性提示，已成为重要的安全评估范式。在此框架内，对抗性提示的多样性对于全面的安全评估至关重要。然而，以往的红队方法往往通过简单的指标（如词频或句子嵌入相似性）追求多样性，这可能无法捕捉攻击策略的有意义变化。此外，训练单一攻击者模型的常见做法限制了对所有潜在攻击风格和风险类别的覆盖。本文介绍了质量-多样性红队（QDRT），这是一个旨在解决这些局限性的新框架。QDRT通过行为条件训练实现目标驱动的多样性，并以开放式方式实现行为重放缓冲。此外，它训练多个专门的攻击者，能够在多样化风格和风险类别中生成高质量攻击。我们的实证评估表明，QDRT生成的攻击在多样性和有效性方面均优于广泛的目标LLMs，包括开源模型\texttt{GPT-2}、\texttt{Llama-3}、\texttt{Gemma-2}、\texttt{Qwen2.5}，以及商业模型\texttt{GPT-4.1}和\texttt{GPT-5-Chat}。这项工作通过提供一种系统有效的自动红队方法，推动了LLM安全领域的发展，最终支持LLMs的负责任部署。我们的代码可在https://github.com/lamda-bbo/QDRT获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical challenge of ensuring the safety and robustness of large language models (LLMs), particularly through the red-teaming framework, which identifies adversarial prompts that provoke harmful responses. Previous methods have relied on simplistic metrics for diversity, such as word frequency and sentence embedding similarity, which fail to capture meaningful variations in attack strategies, and typically utilize a single attacker model, limiting the diversity of attack styles. The proposed Quality-Diversity Red-Teaming (QDRT) framework overcomes these issues by employing behavior-conditioned training and a behavioral replay buffer, allowing for the training of multiple specialized attackers that generate high-quality, diverse attacks across various styles and risk categories. The empirical results demonstrate that QDRT produces attacks that are both more diverse and effective against a range of target LLMs, including both open-source and commercial models, thereby contributing significantly to the field of LLM safety and supporting their responsible deployment.

EvalSafetyGap: A Hybrid Survey and Conceptual Framework for LLM Evaluation-Safety Failures
Authors: Buğra Alperen Uluırmak, Rifat Kurban
First: 2026-06-29T12:33:06+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-31T12:14:44+00:00
Comments: 74 pages, 2 figures, 4 tables. Hybrid systematic survey and conceptual framework on LLM evaluation and AI-safety failures, synthesizing 373 primary studies (2018-2026). Introduces the EvalSafetyGap framework (Instability Decomposition, Alignment Trilemma) and reports an exploratory ten-model audit. Submitted as a review/survey article; not currently under consideration elsewhere
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
This paper presents a systematic survey and conceptual synthesis of the shared measurement problem underlying large language model (LLM) evaluation and AI safety: benchmark scores, reward signals, and safety metrics can improve while the capabilities and alignment properties they are meant to represent remain uncertain. Synthesizing 373 primary studies published between 2018 and 2026, the survey organizes evidence on benchmark validity, contamination, dynamic evaluation, LLM-as-a-judge protocols, adversarial safety testing, reward and proxy optimization, mechanistic interpretability, and AI governance into an eight-stream evidence taxonomy. Building on this synthesis, we introduce EvalSafetyGap, a conceptual framework that unifies benchmark-validity and alignment-failure research as a shared proxy-target divergence problem under optimization pressure, formalized through a Goodhart-inspired Instability Decomposition and an Alignment Trilemma. An exploratory ten-model public-evidence audit illustrates the framework by showing why capability, behavioral robustness, and governance disclosure should be reported as separate evidence layers rather than collapsed into a single safety score. The survey closes with a research agenda for dynamic and contamination-resistant benchmarks, pre-specified multi-attempt threat models, version-locked evaluation, transparent source reporting, and validated mechanistic safety indicators, offering researchers, model developers, and AI auditors a shared vocabulary for measurement-aware LLM safety evaluation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：EvalSafetyGap：大型语言模型评估安全失败的混合调查与概念框架
本文系统性地调查和概念性综合了大型语言模型（LLM）评估和人工智能安全的共享测量问题：基准分数、奖励信号和安全指标可以改善，而它们所代表的能力和对齐属性仍然不确定。通过综合2018年至2026年间发布的373项主要研究，调查将基准有效性、污染、动态评估、LLM作为评判者的协议、对抗性安全测试、奖励和代理优化、机制可解释性以及人工智能治理的证据组织成八个证据分类流。在此基础上，我们引入了EvalSafetyGap，一个概念框架，将基准有效性和对齐失败研究统一为在优化压力下的共享代理目标偏差问题，通过受Goodhart启发的不稳定性分解和对齐三难问题进行形式化。一个探索性的十模型公共证据审计通过展示能力、行为鲁棒性和治理披露为何应作为独立证据层报告，而不是合并为单一安全分数，来说明该框架。调查最后提出了一个研究议程，涉及动态和抗污染基准、预先指定的多次尝试威胁模型、版本锁定评估、透明源报告和验证的机制安全指标，为研究人员、模型开发者和人工智能审计师提供了一个共享的测量意识的LLM安全评估词汇。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenges in evaluating large language models (LLMs) and their safety, highlighting that improvements in benchmark scores and safety metrics do not necessarily correlate with actual capabilities and alignment properties. Previous methods have often failed to adequately address the shared measurement problem, leading to uncertainties in evaluation outcomes. The proposed EvalSafetyGap framework distinguishes itself by integrating benchmark validity and alignment failure into a unified conceptual model, utilizing an Instability Decomposition and an Alignment Trilemma to formalize these issues. This framework is well-motivated as it provides a structured approach to understanding the divergence between proxies and targets in LLM evaluation. The paper contributes a comprehensive survey of 373 studies and an exploratory audit of ten models, demonstrating the necessity of reporting capability, behavioral robustness, and governance disclosure as separate evidence layers, thus supporting the goal of creating more reliable and transparent evaluation methods for LLM safety.
本研究关注在人工智能安全背景下评估大型语言模型（LLM）的关键问题，强调尽管基准分数和安全指标有所改善，但这些指标的可靠性仍然存在不确定性。以往的方法在基准有效性和评估指标污染方面存在困难，导致对模型能力和对齐属性的可靠评估缺乏。提出的EvalSafetyGap框架通过将这些挑战视为共享的代理-目标偏差问题，利用不稳定性分解和对齐三难问题来更好地理解基准与安全之间的关系。本文综合了373项研究的贡献，并引入了LLM评估证据的结构化分类法，最终进行了十个模型的探索性审计，强调了能力、鲁棒性和治理的独立报告需求。这些发现支持了在LLM安全评估中需要更细致和透明的评估过程。
On-Policy Distillation for LLM Safety: A Routing Approach to Template-Robust Realignment
Authors: Yongjian Guo, Wanlun Ma, Lingyu Shen, Xi Xiao, Sheng Wen
First: 2026-07-29T16:07:19+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-29T16:07:19+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Fine-tuning is the dominant paradigm for specializing large language models (LLMs), yet it exposes a critical vulnerability: malicious data providers can embed harmful behaviors into downstream corpora, creating models that retain professional skills while violating human values on demand. Existing safety-realignment defenses often fail in practice due to three key limitations: they frequently cause catastrophic forgetting of specialized skills; their effectiveness collapses when the defender cannot observe the attacker's prompt template; and successfully realigned models remain susceptible to re-jailbreaking via simple system prompt switches. To address these challenges, we propose Routing-based On-Policy Distillation (ROPD), a novel realignment framework that models the divergence between aligned and compromised output probability distributions rather than fitting specific prompt templates. We conduct extensive experiments comparing ROPD against four state-of-the-art baselines across three datasets and three base models with varying alignment strengths. Our results demonstrate that when baseline defenses face template mismatches, often accompanied by severe degradation in downstream task performance. In contrast, ROPD substantially mitigates template-mismatch risks, maintaining superior robustness in both defense effectiveness and capability preservation. While our analysis indicates ROPD is not entirely immune to template shifts, its performance degradation is negligible compared to existing methods, establishing a new standard for robust LLM realignment.
中文标题/摘要
标题：基于策略蒸馏的LLM安全性：一种模板鲁棒重对齐的路由方法
微调是专门化大型语言模型（LLMs）的主流范式，但它暴露了一个关键漏洞：恶意数据提供者可以将有害行为嵌入下游语料库，创建在需求下保留专业技能但违反人类价值观的模型。现有的安全重对齐防御在实践中常常失败，主要有三个关键限制：它们经常导致专业技能的灾难性遗忘；当防御者无法观察攻击者的提示模板时，其有效性崩溃；成功重对齐的模型仍然容易通过简单的系统提示切换重新越狱。为了解决这些挑战，我们提出了基于路由的策略蒸馏（ROPD），这是一种新颖的重对齐框架，它建模对齐和妥协输出概率分布之间的差异，而不是拟合特定的提示模板。我们进行了广泛的实验，将ROPD与四个最先进的基线在三个数据集和三个基础模型上进行比较，后者具有不同的对齐强度。我们的结果表明，当基线防御面临模板不匹配时，通常伴随着下游任务性能的严重下降。相比之下，ROPD显著减轻了模板不匹配的风险，在防御有效性和能力保留方面保持了优越的鲁棒性。尽管我们的分析表明ROPD并非完全免疫于模板变化，但其性能下降与现有方法相比微不足道，为鲁棒的LLM重对齐建立了新的标准。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the vulnerabilities in fine-tuning large language models (LLMs), particularly how malicious data can lead to harmful behaviors while retaining professional skills. Previous methods for safety realignment often suffer from catastrophic forgetting, lack effectiveness against unseen prompt templates, and remain vulnerable to re-jailbreaking. The proposed Routing-based On-Policy Distillation (ROPD) framework differs by focusing on the divergence between aligned and compromised output distributions rather than specific templates, effectively addressing these limitations. The paper contributes a novel approach that demonstrates improved robustness against template mismatches while preserving model capabilities. Extensive experiments show that ROPD outperforms four state-of-the-art baselines across multiple datasets and models, achieving superior defense effectiveness and minimal performance degradation, thus supporting its goals of enhancing LLM safety.

MEDIC: Comprehensive Evaluation of Leading Indicators for LLM Safety and Utility in Clinical Applications
Authors: Praveenkumar Kanithi, Clément Christophe, Marco AF Pimentel, Tathagata Raha, Prateek Munjal, Nada Saadi, Hamza A Javed, Svetlana Maslenkova, Nasir Hayat, Ronnie Rajan, Shadab Khan
First: 2024-09-11T14:44:51+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-28T17:58:21+00:00
Comments: Published in Transactions on Machine Learning Research (06/2026)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
While Large Language Models (LLMs) achieve superhuman performance on standardized medical licensing exams, these static benchmarks have become saturated and increasingly disconnected from the functional requirements of clinical workflows. To bridge the gap between theoretical capability and verified utility, we introduce MEDIC, a comprehensive evaluation framework establishing leading indicators of clinical LLM competence across five dimensions. These upfront indicators reveal cross-benchmark capability gaps, such as the divergence between static knowledge retrieval and functional execution, that inform model selection before costly deployment-based evaluation. Beyond standard question-answering, we assess operational capabilities using deterministic execution protocols and a novel Cross-Examination Framework (CEF), which quantifies information fidelity and hallucination rates without reliance on reference texts. Our evaluation across a heterogeneous task suite exposes critical performance trade-offs: we identify a significant knowledge-execution gap, where proficiency in static retrieval does not predict success in operational tasks such as clinical calculation or SQL generation. Furthermore, we observe a divergence between passive safety (refusal) and active safety (error detection), revealing that models fine-tuned for high refusal rates often fail to reliably audit clinical documentation for factual accuracy. These findings demonstrate that no single architecture dominates across all dimensions, highlighting the necessity of a portfolio approach to clinical model deployment. We accompany this work with a publicly available MEDIC leaderboard at https://hf.co/spaces/m42-health/MEDIC-Benchmark.
中文标题/摘要

The research addresses the limitations of existing evaluation methods for Large Language Models (LLMs) in clinical applications, which often rely on static benchmarks that do not reflect real-world clinical workflows. Previous methods have shown a saturation in performance metrics, failing to capture the functional utility of LLMs in practice. The proposed MEDIC framework introduces a comprehensive evaluation approach that identifies leading indicators of clinical competence across five dimensions, utilizing deterministic execution protocols and a Cross-Examination Framework (CEF) to assess operational capabilities without depending on reference texts. This methodology reveals significant gaps between knowledge retrieval and practical execution, as well as discrepancies between passive and active safety measures in LLMs. The findings indicate that a diverse portfolio of models is necessary for effective clinical deployment, as no single architecture excels across all evaluation dimensions, thus contributing valuable insights for model selection and deployment strategies in healthcare settings.

GPT-Red: Automated Red Teaming via Self-Play at Scale
Authors: Eric Wallace, Christopher A. Choquette-Choo, Nikhil Kandpal, Sam Toyer, Dylan Hunn, Stephanie Lin, Yuxin Wen, Xiangyu Qi, Christopher Wolff, Zizhao Wang, Milad Nasr, Sicheng Zhu, Chuan Guo, Juan Felipe Cerón Uribe, Kaiwen Wang, Aiden Low, Kai Xiao, Kai Chen
First: 2026-07-28T16:03:39+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-28T16:03:39+00:00
Comments: 28 pages.13 main pages and 13 main figures
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
We introduce \textbf{GPT-Red}, an automated red-teaming agent that is trained to discover novel prompt injection attacks against frontier LLMs. The goal of this model is to evaluate and improve the robustness of our production systems. To this end, we use it to adversarially train GPT-5.6, our most robust model to prompt injections to date. To create GPT-Red, we design a scalable self-play algorithm where the model is tasked with attacking a diverse population of simultaneously-trained defender agents. We train the model on realistic red-teaming environments using compute on the same scale as some of our largest RL post-training runs, making it the single-largest LLM safety training run ever documented. GPT-Red excels at red-teaming: it reliably breaks our past models up to GPT-5.5, it finds more successful attacks than human red-teamers, and it generalizes to held-out environments, defender models, and harnesses. In the future, we expect that as we improve the robustness of each new GPT model, it will in turn will provide better learning signal for \textit{even stronger} red-teamer agents, thus unlocking a self-improvement flywheel.
中文标题/摘要
标题：GPT-Red：通过自我对弈实现自动化红队
我们介绍了\textbf{GPT-Red}，一个自动化红队代理，旨在发现针对前沿大型语言模型（LLMs）的新型提示注入攻击。该模型的目标是评估和提高我们生产系统的鲁棒性。为此，我们利用它对GPT-5.6进行对抗性训练，这是迄今为止对提示注入最鲁棒的模型。为了创建GPT-Red，我们设计了一种可扩展的自我对弈算法，模型的任务是攻击一组同时训练的多样化防御代理。我们在现实的红队环境中训练该模型，使用的计算能力与我们一些最大的强化学习后训练运行相当，使其成为有史以来最大的LLM安全训练运行。GPT-Red在红队方面表现出色：它可靠地击破我们过去的模型（最高至GPT-5.5），找到的成功攻击次数超过人类红队员，并且能够推广到未见过的环境、防御模型和工具。在未来，我们预计随着我们提高每个新GPT模型的鲁棒性，它将为\textit{更强大的}红队代理提供更好的学习信号，从而解锁自我改进的飞轮。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the need for improved robustness in large language models (LLMs) against prompt injection attacks, which have been inadequately handled by previous methods that rely on human red-teamers and limited adversarial training. The proposed approach, GPT-Red, utilizes a scalable self-play algorithm to automate the red-teaming process, allowing the model to attack a diverse set of defender agents simultaneously, thus overcoming the limitations of human intervention and enhancing the training environment. This paper contributes by documenting the largest LLM safety training run to date and demonstrating that GPT-Red can effectively identify more successful attacks than human counterparts while generalizing across different environments and models. The methodology involves training GPT-Red in realistic red-teaming scenarios, ultimately leading to the adversarial training of GPT-5.6, which shows significant improvements in robustness against prompt injections, supporting the goal of developing more resilient LLMs.
本研究解决了大型语言模型（LLMs）在抵御提示注入攻击方面的鲁棒性不足的问题，而以往的方法主要依赖人工红队工作，效果不佳。提出的方法GPT-Red采用可扩展的自我对抗算法，自动化红队过程，使模型能够攻击一组同时训练的防御者代理。这种方法具有良好的动机，旨在通过对最强大的模型GPT-5.6进行对抗性训练来增强生产系统的安全性。本文的贡献在于开发了GPT-Red，该模型在发现新攻击方面表现优于人工红队，成功突破了之前的模型（最高至GPT-5.5），并能够在不同环境中进行泛化。该方法论涉及在前所未有的规模上训练真实的红队场景，在LLM安全性和鲁棒性方面取得了显著进展。
TRIDENT: Benchmarking LLM Safety in Finance, Medicine, and Law
Authors: Zheng Hui, Yijiang River Dong, Ehsan Shareghi, Nigel Collier
First: 2025-07-22T17:52:29+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-27T09:44:33+00:00
Comments: COLM 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
As large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in high-risk domains such as law, finance, and medicine, systematically evaluating their domain-specific safety and compliance becomes critical. While prior work has largely focused on improving LLM performance in these domains, it has often neglected the evaluation of domain-specific safety risks. To bridge this gap, we first define domain-specific safety principles for LLMs based on the AMA Principles of Medical Ethics, the ABA Model Rules of Professional Conduct, and the CFA Institute Code of Ethics. Building on this foundation, we introduce Trident-Bench, a benchmark specifically targeting LLM safety in the legal, financial, and medical domains. We evaluated 19 general-purpose and domain-specialized models on Trident-Bench and show that it effectively reveals key safety gaps -- strong generalist models (e.g., GPT, Gemini) can meet basic expectations, whereas domain-specialized models often struggle with subtle ethical nuances. This highlights an urgent need for finer-grained domain-specific safety improvements. By introducing Trident-Bench, our work provides one of the first systematic resources for studying LLM safety in law and finance, and lays the groundwork for future research aimed at reducing the safety risks of deploying LLMs in professionally regulated fields. Code and benchmark will be released at: https://github.com/zackhuiiiii/TRIDENT.

ASEval: Automated Trajectory-Level Security Testing for Autonomous Agents
Authors: Jianan Ma, Xiaohu Du, Ruixiao Lin, Yaoxiang Bian, Jialuo Chen, Yunhao Feng, Xiaofang Yang, Shiwen Cui, Changhua Meng, Xinhao Deng, Jingyi Wang, Zhen Wang
First: 2026-05-21T11:07:51+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-24T06:09:29+00:00
Comments: 18 pages, 12 figures, 8 tables. Code and data available at https://github.com/antgroup/Agent3Sigma-Stage
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
As autonomous agents (e.g., OpenClaw) increasingly operate with deep system-level privileges to execute complex tasks, they introduce severe, unmitigated security risks. Existing LLM safety testing methods are largely built around prompt-level inputs and response-level judgments, while recent agent benchmarks remain limited in automation, trajectory coverage, and action-grounded judgment. In this work, we present ASEval, a novel automated security testing framework for autonomous agents. ASEval generates realistic benign multi-turn conversations, perturbs them into risk test cases through carefully designed perturbation operators, executes each case against the target agent, and applies an action-grounded oracle to judge the complete trace. We instantiate ASEval to construct A3S-Bench, a test suite of 2,254 executable multi-turn test cases spanning diverse usage scenarios, delivery patterns, and agent-level security risks. Extensive evaluation on 11 LLM-backed OpenClaw agents confirms that ASEval effectively exposes pervasive security failures. In particular, our proposed perturbation operators raise the average risk-trigger rate from 22.9% under existing injection techniques to 47.4%, as judged by an action-grounded oracle that matches human annotation at 93.2% binary F1. Our further analysis and case studies illustrate that such failures emerge from multi-turn delivery, persistent state, and tool-mediated actions that response-level evaluation overlooks.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ASEval：自动化轨迹级安全测试框架用于自主代理
随着自主代理（例如，OpenClaw）越来越多地以深层系统级权限执行复杂任务，它们引入了严重且未缓解的安全风险。现有的LLM安全测试方法主要围绕提示级输入和响应级判断构建，而最近的代理基准在自动化、轨迹覆盖和基于动作的判断方面仍然有限。在本研究中，我们提出了ASEval，一个新颖的自主代理自动化安全测试框架。ASEval生成现实的良性多轮对话，通过精心设计的扰动操作将其扰动为风险测试用例，针对目标代理执行每个用例，并应用基于动作的oracle来判断完整的轨迹。我们实例化ASEval构建了A3S-Bench，一个包含2254个可执行多轮测试用例的测试套件，涵盖多种使用场景、交付模式和代理级安全风险。对11个基于LLM的OpenClaw代理的广泛评估确认ASEval有效暴露了普遍的安全失败。特别是，我们提出的扰动操作将现有注入技术下的平均风险触发率从22.9%提高到47.4%，根据与人类注释匹配的93.2%二元F1的基于动作的oracle进行判断。我们的进一步分析和案例研究表明，这些失败源于多轮交付、持久状态和工具介导的动作，而响应级评估则忽视了这些因素。

The proposed ASEval framework differs by generating realistic multi-turn conversations and perturbing them into risk test cases, which are then executed against the target agent, allowing for action-grounded judgments of the entire interaction trace. This approach is well-motivated as it aims to enhance the detection of security failures that traditional methods overlook. The paper contributes by introducing ASEval and constructing the A3S-Bench test suite, which includes 2,254 executable test cases that cover various scenarios and security risks. Evaluation on 11 LLM-backed OpenClaw agents demonstrates that ASEval significantly improves risk detection, raising the average risk-trigger rate from 22.9% to 47.4%, with a high correlation to human judgment, thus supporting its goals of enhancing security testing for autonomous agents.

Geometric Configurations of Perturbed Jailbreak Prompts
Authors: Lynn Delcon, Andres Algaba, Vincent Ginis
First: 2026-07-22T11:52:43+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-22T11:52:43+00:00
Comments: 21 pages, 9 figures, 7 tables, 2nd Workshop on Safe AI (SafeAI)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Perturbation techniques that turn unsuccessful jailbreak prompts into successful ones are continuously evolving, constituting a major security threat to LLM safety. In this paper, we investigate the internal representations of such string-level perturbed jailbreak inputs in the small weight models of the Qwen-2.5-1.5B/-3B/-7B-Instruct and Llama-3.2-1B/-3B/-3.1-8B-Instruct families. We select two representation spaces: the last-layer-last-token embedding space and the top-50 next-token probability space. The former space separates prompts based on their spelling and format, while the latter space is effectively one-dimensional but appears more complex to cluster. Within our refusal-dominated answer set we find no behavioral hyperplane in either space. Only the next token "Sure" in the 1.5B Qwen model, and both tokens "," and "ĊĊ" in the 1$ Llama model, display a significant association with a compliant-labeled answer.
中文标题/摘要
标题：扰动越狱提示的几何配置
扰动技术将不成功的越狱提示转变为成功的提示，持续演变，构成对大型语言模型安全的重大威胁。本文研究了Qwen-2.5-1.5B/-3B/-7B-Instruct和Llama-3.2-1B/-3B/-3.1-8B-Instruct系列的小权重模型中此类字符串级扰动越狱输入的内部表示。我们选择了两个表示空间：最后一层最后一个标记的嵌入空间和前50个下一个标记的概率空间。前者空间根据提示的拼写和格式进行区分，而后者空间有效地是一维的，但在聚类时看起来更复杂。在我们的拒绝主导的答案集中，我们在任一空间中都未发现行为超平面。只有在1.5B Qwen模型中，下一个标记“Sure”，以及在1$ Llama模型中，两个标记“,”和“ĊĊ”与合规标记的答案显示出显著关联。
Summary / 总结

Previous methods have focused on analyzing prompt effectiveness without adequately understanding the internal representations of these perturbed inputs, leading to gaps in security measures. This paper proposes a novel approach by examining the internal representations of perturbed jailbreak prompts in specific embedding spaces of the Qwen and Llama model families, revealing that the last-layer-last-token embedding space and the next-token probability space provide distinct insights into prompt behavior. The methodology involves analyzing these representation spaces to assess the models' responses to perturbations, ultimately demonstrating that the proposed approach effectively highlights vulnerabilities in LLMs, supporting the goal of enhancing their safety against jailbreak attempts.

Stress Testing Concept Erasure with Large Language Model Agents
Authors: Yuyang Xue, Feng Chen, Zhihua Liu, Edward Moroshko, Jingyu Sun, Steven McDonagh, Sotirios A. Tsaftaris
First: 2026-07-20T12:38:50+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-22T09:45:01+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Concept erasure aims to remove semantic concepts from a trained generative model and is increasingly important for responsible AI deployment. However, verifying whether a model has robustly removed targeted concepts remains a critical challenge. Existing evaluation methods are typically pre-defined and static, failing to expose vulnerabilities under diverse natural-language probes and challenging conditions. Moreover, manually designed evaluation strategies can be biased and difficult to scale. We posit that concept erasure evaluation is best formulated as an adaptive hypothesis search, operationalised by agents that iteratively propose, critique, and verify tests to systematically expand coverage of failure modes. To this end, we propose Stress Testing Agents for Concept Erasure (STACE), a framework that autonomously stress-tests concept-erased models using multiple Large Language Model (LLM) agents, by iteratively generating and verifying stress-testing hypotheses grounded by external knowledge. We also introduce a suite of metrics for assessing the performance and efficiency of LLM-agent-powered stress-testing frameworks. Our extensive experiments show that STACE outperforms five LLM-based evaluation baselines on four concept categories. Further analysis across two T2I models, six concept erasure approaches, and various erasure strengths show that STACE is robust for different settings. We also show that STACE can be adapted beyond concept erasure evaluation to other problem domains, such as LLM jailbreaking. Our code is available anonymously.
中文标题/摘要
标题：大型语言模型代理的概念消除压力测试
概念消除旨在从训练好的生成模型中移除语义概念，对于负责任的人工智能部署越来越重要。然而，验证模型是否稳健地移除了目标概念仍然是一个关键挑战。现有的评估方法通常是预定义和静态的，未能在多样的自然语言探测和挑战条件下暴露出脆弱性。此外，手动设计的评估策略可能存在偏见且难以扩展。我们认为，概念消除评估最好被表述为一种自适应假设搜索，由代理迭代提出、批评和验证测试，以系统性地扩展失败模式的覆盖范围。为此，我们提出了概念消除压力测试代理（STACE），这是一个框架，通过多个大型语言模型（LLM）代理自主地对概念消除模型进行压力测试，迭代生成和验证基于外部知识的压力测试假设。我们还引入了一套用于评估LLM代理驱动的压力测试框架性能和效率的指标。我们的广泛实验表明，STACE在四个概念类别上优于五个基于LLM的评估基线。对两个T2I模型、六种概念消除方法和各种消除强度的进一步分析表明，STACE在不同设置下都表现稳健。我们还展示了STACE可以适应于概念消除评估以外的其他问题领域，如LLM越狱。我们的代码可匿名获取。

Operational Hallucination and Safety Drift in AI Agents
Authors: Shasha Yu, Fiona Carroll, Barry L. Bentley
First: 2026-07-20T17:01:50+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-20T17:01:50+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) serving as planners in tool-using autonomous agents introduce dynamic reliability risks in multi-turn execution. While single-turn safety mechanisms are relatively mature, extended interactions reveal structural vulnerabilities where initial alignment degrades over time. This paper empirically characterizes two observed failure modes across multiple state-of-the-art LLMs: Safety Drift, the gradual erosion of declared safety intent leading to constraint-violating actions (e.g., textual refusal followed by reconnaissance and unsafe execution), and Operational Hallucination, persistent repetitive tool calls indicative of flawed state perception (e.g., livelocks even in legitimate tasks). Through controlled multi-turn evaluation on high-stakes ethical dilemmas, malicious requests, and benign controls, we quantify these phenomena using declaration-action gap and livelock metrics, demonstrating their cross-model prevalence under direct execution protocols. Root-cause analysis attributes the instabilities to the decoupling of reasoning context from execution state in current agent loops. We propose an Action-Aware Supervision Layer - a lightweight, plug-and-play architectural blueprint incorporating intent-action consistency checks, runtime state tracking, and forced termination primitives. Post-hoc simulation on captured failure trajectories shows the layer can intercept observed violations without false positives on benign cases. This work advances agent reliability by shifting focus from linguistic safeguards to enforceable architectural mechanisms for responsible agentic AI.
中文标题/摘要
标题：人工智能代理中的操作性幻觉与安全漂移
作为工具使用自主代理的规划者，大型语言模型（LLMs）在多轮执行中引入了动态可靠性风险。虽然单轮安全机制相对成熟，但扩展交互揭示了结构性脆弱性，初始对齐随着时间的推移而下降。本文实证描述了多个最先进LLMs中观察到的两种失败模式：安全漂移，即声明的安全意图逐渐侵蚀，导致违反约束的行为（例如，文本拒绝后进行侦察和不安全执行）；操作性幻觉，即持续重复的工具调用，表明状态感知存在缺陷（例如，即使在合法任务中也出现活锁）。通过对高风险伦理困境、恶意请求和良性控制的受控多轮评估，我们使用声明-行动差距和活锁指标量化这些现象，展示它们在直接执行协议下的跨模型普遍性。根本原因分析将不稳定性归因于当前代理循环中推理上下文与执行状态的解耦。我们提出了一种行动感知监督层——一种轻量级、即插即用的架构蓝图，包含意图-行动一致性检查、运行时状态跟踪和强制终止原语。对捕获的失败轨迹进行事后模拟表明，该层可以在良性案例中拦截观察到的违规行为而不产生假阳性。该工作通过将重点从语言保障转向可执行的架构机制，推动了代理的可靠性，以实现负责任的代理人工智能。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the reliability risks posed by large language models (LLMs) when used as planners in autonomous agents, particularly during multi-turn interactions where initial safety alignment can degrade. Previous methods have focused on single-turn safety mechanisms, which do not account for the structural vulnerabilities that arise over extended interactions, leading to issues such as Safety Drift and Operational Hallucination. This paper contributes by empirically characterizing these failure modes and proposing an Action-Aware Supervision Layer that enhances agent reliability through intent-action consistency checks and runtime state tracking. The methodology involves controlled multi-turn evaluations on ethical dilemmas and malicious requests, demonstrating that the proposed layer can effectively intercept violations without generating false positives, thus supporting the goal of creating more reliable AI agents.
本文探讨了作为规划者的语言模型（LLMs）在自主代理中使用时所面临的可靠性风险，特别关注在多轮交互中出现的安全漂移和操作幻觉问题。以往的方法建立了单轮安全机制，但在延长交互中未能保持一致性，导致约束违反和状态感知错误。所提出的方法引入了一个行动感知监督层，通过整合意图-行动一致性检查和运行时状态跟踪，增强了代理的可靠性，有效解决了识别出的脆弱性。研究方法涉及对伦理困境和其他场景的控制多轮评估，揭示了这些失败模式在多种LLM中的普遍性。研究结果表明，该层能够成功拦截违规行为而不产生误报，从而支持创建更可靠和负责任的人工智能代理的目标。
Adaptive Adversaries: A Multi-Turn, Multi-LLM Benchmark for LLM Agent Security
Authors: Devina Jain, David Hartmann, Chuan Li
First: 2026-07-20T15:30:38+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-20T15:30:38+00:00
Comments: Second Workshop on Agents in the Wild: Safety, Security, and Beyond
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
LLM-based agents process external content, exposing them to prompt injection and multi-turn manipulation. Most safety benchmarks evaluate defenders against fixed attack pools collected before evaluation, single-turn or multi-turn. We present a 21-scenario benchmark for \emph{adaptive multi-round attacks against memoryless LLM defenders}: an autonomous LLM attacker observes prior defender responses and pivots across rounds, while each defender response is evaluated as a fresh interaction. Holding the 21 scenarios, attackers, defenders, and structured-output scoring fixed, restricting scoring to the first attacker turn yields $0$-$1\%$ attack success rate (ASR); allowing 15 rounds of adaptive attack yields $5.4$-$14.0\%$. Pooling three frontier attacker LLMs uncovers $1.4$-$2.2\times$ as many unique successful attacks as the best single attacker, and the generated attacks have low cosine similarity ($0.02$-$0.14$) to attacks in existing benchmarks. Claude Opus 4.6 and GPT-5.4 are tied in aggregate ($5.4\%$ each; overlapping $95\%$ CIs), but their weaknesses differ sharply: on one scenario Opus reaches $60\%$ ASR ($95\%$ CI $36$--$80\%$) while GPT-5.4 and Gemini each stay at $7\%$ (CI $1$-$30\%$; the gap is preserved in a higher-$N$ replication). $13$ of $21$ scenarios distinguish at least one defender pair, yet rankings disagree across scenarios (Kendall's $W = 0.19$). We release the benchmark -- 21 evaluation scenarios, 10 public development scenarios, the orchestrator, baseline harnesses, and a multi-attacker CLI -- plus 945 transcripts from the 3$\times$3 frontier matrix, an attack-replay dataset, and 18{,}422 gpt-oss-20b battles from an open competition's final scoring rounds.
中文标题/摘要
标题：自适应对手：针对LLM代理安全的多轮、多LLM基准测试
基于LLM的代理处理外部内容，使其暴露于提示注入和多轮操控中。大多数安全基准测试评估防御者对固定攻击池的抵御能力，这些攻击池在评估前收集，分为单轮或多轮。我们提出了一个21场景的基准，用于\emph{针对无记忆LLM防御者的自适应多轮攻击}：自主LLM攻击者观察先前防御者的响应并在轮次间调整，而每个防御者的响应被评估为一次新的交互。在保持21个场景、攻击者、防御者和结构化输出评分固定的情况下，将评分限制在第一个攻击者回合，攻击成功率（ASR）为$0$-$1\%$；允许15轮自适应攻击则产生$5.4$-$14.0\%$的成功率。汇聚三种前沿攻击者LLM发现成功攻击的独特数量是最佳单一攻击者的$1.4$-$2.2$倍，且生成的攻击与现有基准中的攻击的余弦相似度较低（$0.02$-$0.14$）。Claude Opus 4.6和GPT-5.4在总计上相同（各为$5.4\%$；重叠$95\%$置信区间），但它们的弱点差异明显：在一个场景中，Opus达到$60\%$的ASR（$95\%$ CI $36$--$80\%$），而GPT-5.4和Gemini各保持在$7\%$（CI $1$-$30\%$；在更高的$N$复制中保持差距）。$21$个场景中有$13$个区分至少一对防御者，但场景间的排名不一致（Kendall's $W = 0.19$）。我们发布了基准——21个评估场景、10个公共开发场景、协调器、基线工具和多攻击者CLI——以及来自3$\times$3前沿矩阵的945个转录、一个攻击重放数据集和来自开放竞赛最终评分轮的18,422场gpt-oss-20b对战。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the vulnerabilities of LLM-based agents to prompt injection and multi-turn manipulation, highlighting the limitations of existing safety benchmarks that typically evaluate against fixed attack pools in single or multi-turn scenarios. The proposed approach introduces a novel 21-scenario benchmark for adaptive multi-round attacks, where an autonomous LLM attacker can observe and adapt based on previous defender responses, contrasting with traditional methods that do not account for such adaptability. This methodology is well-motivated as it reflects real-world attack dynamics, contributing significantly to the field by revealing that allowing adaptive attacks increases the attack success rate from nearly 0% to between 5.4% and 14.0%. The paper demonstrates that pooling multiple advanced attacker LLMs yields a greater variety of successful attacks compared to single attackers, with performance metrics indicating that the proposed benchmark effectively distinguishes between different defender capabilities across various scenarios.
本研究关注基于LLM的代理在提示注入和多轮操控方面的脆弱性，强调现有安全基准在评估防御者时对静态攻击池的依赖以及通常只在单轮场景下进行评估的局限性。提出的方法引入了一个21场景的基准，用于自适应多轮攻击，其中自主LLM攻击者可以根据先前防御者的响应观察和调整其策略，从而提供对防御者性能的更动态评估。这种方法的动机充分，因为它比以前的方法更准确地反映了现实世界的互动。本文的贡献在于发布了一个全面的基准，包括各种评估场景和数据集，允许对LLM防御者安全性进行更彻底的评估。实验结果表明，允许15轮自适应攻击显著提高了攻击成功率至5.4%-14.0%，相比之下，固定攻击的成功率仅为0%-1%，证明了所提方法在揭示LLM防御者脆弱性方面的有效性。
STAC: When Innocent Tools Form Dangerous Chains for LLM Agents
Authors: Jing-Jing Li, Jianfeng He, Chao Shang, Devang Kulshreshtha, Xun Xian, Yi Zhang, Hang Su, Sandesh Swamy, Yanjun Qi
First: 2025-09-30T00:31:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-19T03:06:33+00:00
Comments: Long version with 15 pages in main draft and a total of 39 pages including references and appendix. Data and code \url{https://github.com/amazon-science/MultiTurnAgentAttack}
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
As LLMs advance into autonomous agents with tool-use capabilities, they introduce security challenges that extend beyond traditional content-based LLM safety concerns. This paper introduces Sequential Tool Attack Chaining (\STAC), a novel multi-turn attack framework that exploits agent tool use. \STAC chains together tool calls that each appear harmless in isolation but, when combined, collectively enable harmful operations that only become apparent at the final execution step. At the core of \STAC is an automated, closed-loop pipeline that synthesizes executable multi-step tool chains, validates them through in-environment execution, and reverse-engineers stealthy multi-turn prompts that reliably induce agents to execute the verified malicious sequence. Using this framework, we generate and systematically evaluate 483 \STAC cases, featuring 1,352 sets of user-agent-environment interactions and spanning diverse domains, tasks, agent types, and 10 failure modes. Our evaluations show that state-of-the-art LLM agents are highly vulnerable to \STAC, with an average final attack success rate (ASR) of 91.2\% -- exceeding 90\% for all but one of the eight agents evaluated. We further perform defense analysis and find that existing prompt-based defenses provide limited protection. To address this gap, we propose a new reasoning-driven defense prompt that achieves the strongest initial-turn protection, cutting ASR by up to 28.8\%; however, this advantage erodes sharply under adaptive attacks, and an experience-based defense (ToolShield) proves more durable over sustained multi-turn interactions. These results highlight a crucial gap: defending tool-enabled agents requires reasoning over entire action sequences and their cumulative effects, rather than evaluating isolated prompts or responses.
中文标题/摘要
标题：STAC：当无辜工具形成对LLM代理的危险链条
随着LLM向具备工具使用能力的自主代理发展，它们引入了超越传统内容基础LLM安全问题的安全挑战。本文介绍了顺序工具攻击链（STAC），一种新颖的多轮攻击框架，利用代理的工具使用。STAC将看似无害的工具调用串联在一起，但当组合时，集体启用有害操作，这些操作仅在最终执行步骤时显现。STAC的核心是一个自动化的闭环管道，合成可执行的多步骤工具链，通过环境执行验证它们，并逆向工程隐秘的多轮提示，可靠地诱导代理执行经过验证的恶意序列。利用该框架，我们生成并系统评估了483个STAC案例，涉及1,352组用户-代理-环境交互，涵盖多种领域、任务、代理类型和10种失败模式。我们的评估显示，最先进的LLM代理对STAC高度脆弱，平均最终攻击成功率（ASR）为91.2%，在评估的八个代理中，除了一个外，均超过90%。我们进一步进行防御分析，发现现有的基于提示的防御提供的保护有限。为了解决这一问题，我们提出了一种新的基于推理的防御提示，实现了最强的初始回合保护，将ASR降低了多达28.8%；然而，在自适应攻击下，这一优势迅速减弱，而基于经验的防御（ToolShield）在持续的多轮交互中表现出更强的耐久性。这些结果突显了一个关键差距：防御工具启用的代理需要对整个动作序列及其累积效果进行推理，而不是评估孤立的提示或响应。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the emerging security challenges posed by large language models (LLMs) as they evolve into autonomous agents capable of tool use, which introduces risks beyond traditional content-based safety concerns. Previous methods primarily focused on isolated prompt evaluations, which fail to account for the cumulative effects of tool interactions, leading to vulnerabilities in LLM agents. The proposed Sequential Tool Attack Chaining (STAC) framework offers a novel approach by automating the synthesis of multi-step tool chains that appear harmless individually but can execute harmful operations when combined. This methodology includes an automated, closed-loop pipeline for generating and validating these tool chains, resulting in 483 STAC cases with a high average attack success rate of 91.2% across various agent types and tasks. Addressing Benchmarking Gaps in Large Language Models for Health and Medicine with Dynamic Red-Teaming
Authors: Jiazhen Pan, Bailiang Jian, Paul Hager, Yundi Zhang, Che Liu, Friederike Jungmann, Hongwei Bran Li, Julian Canisius, Chenyu You, Junde Wu, Jiayuan Zhu, Fenglin Liu, Yuyuan Liu, Niklas Bubeck, Moritz Knolle, Chen, Chen, Christian Wachinger, Zhenyu Gong, Cheng Ouyang, Georgios Kaissis, Benedikt Wiestler, Daniel Rueckert
First: 2025-07-30T08:44:22+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-15T15:41:30+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to answer health-related questions and support healthcare workflows, yet evidence for their safety still relies heavily on static benchmarks that can rapidly become obsolete or be optimized against. Here we introduce a Dynamic, Automatic, and Systematic (DAS) red-teaming audit framework that continuously stress-tests LLMs for health across four safety-critical axes: robustness, privacy, bias/fairness, and hallucination/factual inaccuracies. Validated against board-certified clinicians with high concordance, a suite of adversarial agents autonomously mutates health-related test cases to uncover vulnerabilities in real time. Applying DAS to 15 proprietary and open-source LLMs revealed a profound gap between high static benchmark performance and low dynamic reliability--the "Benchmarking Gap". Despite median MedQA accuracy exceeding 80\%, 94\% of previously correct answers failed under dynamic robustness testing. This brittleness generalized to the realistic, open-ended HealthBench dataset, where top-tier models exhibited failure rates exceeding 70\% and sharp shifts in model rankings across evaluations, suggesting that high scores on established static benchmarks may reflect superficial memorization. We observed similarly high failure rates across other domains: privacy leaks were elicited in 86\% of scenarios, cognitive-bias priming altered recommendations in 81\% of fairness tests, and hallucination rates exceeded 74\% in widely used models. By converting LLM safety evaluation for health from a static checklist into a living adversarial audit, DAS provides a scalable framework for surfacing latent risks before such systems are deployed in consumer-facing health assistants, clinician-facing tools, and broader healthcare workflows. Code is available at https://github.com/JZPeterPan/DAS-Medical-Red-Teaming-Agents.

标题：通过动态红队解决大型语言模型在健康和医学中的基准测试差距

The Joint Effect of Quantization and Sampling Temperature on LLM Safety Alignment: A Factorial Analysis
Authors: Hari Prasad, Ritam Pal
First: 2026-06-28T19:52:25+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-15T10:24:21+00:00
Comments: 11 pages, 5 Figures
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Modern LLM deployments often combine quantization with higher sampling temperatures to reduce cost, latency, or repetition, yet safety evaluations usually treat these as fixed implementation details. We test whether models that are safe at FP16 with greedy decoding remain safe after quantization and stochastic sampling, or whether the two factors amplify each other. We evaluate 8 instruction-tuned models from five families across 3 precisions and 6 temperatures, covering 144 configurations on 7 harmfulness benchmarks and generating about 2.0 million responses, which are scored by a six-judge safety ensemble. Contrary to concerns that low-bit deployment erodes alignment, we find that standard quantization is approximately safety-neutral: for 7 of 8 models, AWQ INT4 keeps attack success within about 1.6 percentage points of FP16 or lowers it, with clear degradation only for SmolLM3-3B (34.5% to 44.1%). However, the larger risk comes from sampling: higher temperatures sharply increase decision instability, with DFR reaching 41.9% at T = 1.0, even when average ASR changes only modestly. The two factors do not compound: our Compound Degradation Index remains sub-additive (-0.071 to +0.018), indicating that quantization partially offsets rather than amplifies temperature-induced degradation. Finally, a per-benchmark breakdown shows that single-benchmark evaluation badly understates risk: several models scoring 0% on AdvBench exceed 80% on ManyHarm. Standard INT4/INT8 quantization can therefore be reasonable for well-aligned models, but safety claims should report multi-sample stability across multiple benchmarks rather than rely on a single benchmark at greedy decoding.
中文标题/摘要
标题：量化与采样温度对大型语言模型安全对齐的联合影响：一个因子分析
现代大型语言模型的部署通常将量化与更高的采样温度结合，以降低成本、延迟或重复性，但安全评估通常将这些视为固定的实施细节。我们测试在FP16下使用贪婪解码安全的模型在量化和随机采样后是否仍然安全，或者这两个因素是否相互放大。我们评估了来自五个家族的8个指令调优模型，涵盖3种精度和6种温度，共144种配置，在7个有害性基准上生成约200万条响应，并由六名评审组成的安全团队进行评分。与低位部署会削弱对齐的担忧相反，我们发现标准量化大致是安全中性的：对于8个模型中的7个，AWQ INT4将攻击成功率保持在FP16的约1.6个百分点以内或降低，只有SmolLM3-3B出现明显退化（34.5%降至44.1%）。然而，较大的风险来自采样：更高的温度显著增加决策不稳定性，在T = 1.0时DFR达到41.9%，即使平均ASR变化仅适度。这两个因素并不相互叠加：我们的复合退化指数保持在亚加性范围内（-0.071至+0.018），表明量化部分抵消而非放大温度引起的退化。最后，逐基准的细分显示，单基准评估严重低估了风险：几个在AdvBench上得分为0%的模型在ManyHarm上超过80%。因此，标准INT4/INT8量化对于对齐良好的模型是合理的，但安全声明应报告多个基准的多样本稳定性，而不是依赖于贪婪解码的单一基准。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the safety alignment of large language models (LLMs) when subjected to quantization and varying sampling temperatures, which are common practices in modern deployments aimed at reducing costs and latency. Previous methods often treated these factors as fixed, leading to concerns about their combined effects on model safety. The proposed approach systematically evaluates the interaction between quantization and sampling temperature across multiple models and configurations, revealing that while quantization is generally safety-neutral, higher sampling temperatures significantly increase decision instability. The study contributes by demonstrating that quantization does not exacerbate safety risks and that safety evaluations should consider multi-benchmark stability rather than relying on single-benchmark assessments. The methodology involves factorial analysis of 144 configurations across 8 instruction-tuned models, yielding insights that support the conclusion that standard quantization can be safe for well-aligned models, provided that comprehensive safety evaluations are conducted.

Minionese: Comprehensive Benchmark and Mechanistic Study of Multilingual LLM Safety
Authors: Chigozirim Ifebi, Brent Kong, Ayushi Mehrotra
First: 2026-07-11T04:13:42+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-11T04:13:42+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Safety alignment in large language models remains brittle across languages: prompts reliably refused in English can elicit harmful compliance in non-English and low-resource settings. We introduce \textsc{Minionese}, a multilingual jailbreak benchmark spanning 18 languages, 4 resource tiers, and 4 perturbation types (standard translation, code-switching, transliteration, and translationese), paired with a geometric mechanistic analysis of refusal failure across language tiers. We show that each attack type produces a distinct vulnerability profile: transliteration vulnerability is mediated by script identity, code-switching maintains effectiveness through the lowest-resource tier, and a sharp safety regime transition between Tiers 2 and 3 is consistent across all models. Mechanistically, low-resource jailbreaks succeed by routing harmful content through a geometrically misaligned subspace that projects insufficiently onto the refusal directions, leaving the refusal mechanism intact but untriggered. These findings show that English-only safety evaluations are insufficient; they require accounting for script family, perturbation type, and per-language alignment coverage. The benchmark and analysis code is at https://github.com/Brentkong/Minionese-Comprehensive-Benchmark-and-Mechanistic-Study-of-Multilingual-LLM-Safety.git.

Mechanistic Interpretability of LLM Jailbreaks via Internal Attribution Graphs
Authors: Anupam Wagle, Ifrat Ikhtear Uddin, Chaowei Zhang, Longwei Wang
First: 2026-07-08T20:31:06+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-08T20:31:06+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) exhibit remarkable capabilities but remain highly vulnerable to adversarial prompts and jailbreak attacks. Existing approaches primarily analyze these failures through input-output behaviors or attribution methods, offering limited insight into how adversarial perturbations alter the model's internal reasoning. Consequently, the mechanisms underlying unsafe or incorrect behaviors remain poorly understood. We introduce a mechanistic framework for diagnosing LLM vulnerabilities using paired internal computation graphs, which represent prompt-specific inference as structured causal interactions among latent features. By constructing and aligning computation graphs for clean and attacked prompts, we reveal that adversarial attacks induce systematic transformations of internal reasoning, including suppression of safety-relevant components, emergence of attack-specific features, and rerouting of computation paths. Building on this representation, we propose a unified framework that (i) decomposes computation into invariant, suppressed, and emergent structures, (ii) identifies recurring vulnerability motifs associated with failure modes, and (iii) performs causal interventions on nodes, paths, and subgraphs to directly evaluate their contributions to attack success. This enables a transition from descriptive attribution to causal diagnosis of model failures. Experiments across multiple open-source LLMs and diverse adversarial and jailbreak benchmarks demonstrate that structural deviations in internal computation graphs strongly correlate with unsafe behaviors. Furthermore, targeted interventions on identified vulnerability motifs improve model robustness, establishing internal computation graphs as a principled foundation for understanding, diagnosing, and mitigating LLM vulnerabilities.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过内部归因图对LLM越狱的机制性可解释性
大型语言模型（LLMs）展现出显著的能力，但仍然对对抗性提示和越狱攻击高度脆弱。现有方法主要通过输入输出行为或归因方法分析这些失败，提供的见解有限，无法揭示对抗性扰动如何改变模型的内部推理。因此，导致不安全或不正确行为的机制仍然不清楚。我们引入了一种机制框架，通过配对的内部计算图来诊断LLM的脆弱性，这些图将特定提示的推理表示为潜在特征之间的结构因果交互。通过构建和对齐干净和攻击提示的计算图，我们揭示了对抗性攻击引发的内部推理的系统性转变，包括安全相关组件的抑制、攻击特定特征的出现和计算路径的重新引导。在此表示的基础上，我们提出了一个统一框架，(i) 将计算分解为不变、抑制和新兴结构，(ii) 识别与失败模式相关的重复脆弱性特征，(iii) 对节点、路径和子图进行因果干预，以直接评估它们对攻击成功的贡献。这使得从描述性归因转向模型失败的因果诊断成为可能。针对多个开源LLM和多样的对抗性及越狱基准的实验表明，内部计算图的结构偏差与不安全行为强烈相关。此外，对识别出的脆弱性特征进行有针对性的干预提高了模型的鲁棒性，确立了内部计算图作为理解、诊断和减轻LLM脆弱性的原则基础。

Predicting LLM Safety Before Release by Simulating Deployment
Authors: Marcus Williams, Hannah Sheahan, Cameron Raymond, Tomek Korbak, Deng Pan, Peilin Yang, Leon Maksin, Ningyi Xie, Phillip Guo, Ian Kivlichan, Micah Carroll
First: 2026-07-08T09:17:47+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-08T09:17:47+00:00
Comments: 31 pages
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Pre-deployment safety evaluations aim to inform the downstream risks of releasing a new AI model. Yet most evaluations provide limited evidence about how often undesired model behavior will occur in deployment: they generally have insufficient coverage, are unrepresentative, and are generally recognizable as tests. To address these concerns, we study a simple way to simulate a model deployment: starting from de-identified conversations from a previous model deployment, we hold fixed the initial conversation prefix and regenerate the next response using a candidate model. The resulting responses can then both be audited for novel misalignments and used to estimate the prevalence of model misbehavior before deployment. We evaluate deployment simulation across four GPT-5-series deployments, using registered, outcome-blinded predictions for GPT-5.4 and retrospective analyses of three earlier releases. We find that deployment simulation produces informative estimates of post-deployment misbehavior rates and outperforms baselines based on adversarially selected production data; its evaluation-awareness point estimates were also much closer to production traffic than those from traditional evaluations. We also identify the realism of tool resampling as a central challenge for further improving predictions and share results suggesting that this challenge is surmountable even in complex tool-use settings. Finally, we show that deployment simulation can be seeded from public chat datasets and remain informative about production misbehavior rates, suggesting a path for external researchers to run deployment-grounded evaluations without access to private production logs. Overall, deployment simulation helps evaluators forecast how language models will behave in the real world and supports more quantitative assessment of deployment risk.

Operational Reframing and Approval-Framed Delegation in Multi-Agent LLM Safety
Authors: Lifei Liu, Haoran Yu, Xiaochong Jiang, Su Wang, Pin Qian, Yihang Chen
First: 2026-07-08T07:31:37+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-08T07:31:37+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety evaluations of multi-agent LLM systems often compare a direct prompt with a planner-executor pipeline and report the difference as a single "pipeline effect." We argue that this aggregate is difficult to interpret because it conflates three mechanisms: harmful intent may be reframed as plausible operational work, the planner may refuse or transform the request, and the executor may act under delegation prompts implying prior approval. To separate these factors, we introduce a five-condition controlled contrast design, evaluated on 30 synthetic harmful scenarios and an exploratory external validation set from four agent-safety benchmarks using LLM-judged compliance. Our results show that aggregate pipeline safety is not a stable architectural property. Operational reframing is the most portable risk signal, increasing compliance for GPT, Gemini, and DeepSeek across both scenario sets, while Claude is comparatively resistant. Planner behavior can offset this risk mainly through refusal; however, when the planner produces executable steps, the executor may become more compliant than under the direct operational baseline. Approval-framed delegation is sensitive to prompt design, model pairing, and scenario source, and a skeptical executor prompt sharply reduces compliance. Raw-direct model rankings can also mispredict deployed planner-executor behavior. Gemini is safest under raw direct prompts in the primary set yet shows the largest amplification with a Claude planner, rising from 8.9 percent to 38.9 percent compliance. GPTs near-zero aggregate pipeline effect instead hides a reframing increase canceled by planner refusal. These findings suggest that multi-agent safety evaluations should report reframing, planner behavior, delegation framing, and model pairing separately before attributing failures to architecture itself.
中文标题/摘要
标题：多智能体LLM安全中的操作重构与批准框架委托
多智能体LLM系统的安全评估通常将直接提示与规划-执行管道进行比较，并将差异报告为单一的“管道效应”。我们认为，这种汇总难以解释，因为它混淆了三种机制：有害意图可能被重构为合理的操作工作，规划者可能拒绝或转变请求，执行者可能在暗示先前批准的委托提示下行动。为了分离这些因素，我们引入了一个五条件的对照设计，在30个合成有害场景和来自四个代理安全基准的探索性外部验证集上进行评估，使用LLM判断的合规性。我们的结果表明，汇总管道安全并不是一个稳定的架构属性。操作重构是最具可移植性的风险信号，在两个场景集上提高了GPT、Gemini和DeepSeek的合规性，而Claude则相对抗拒。规划者的行为主要通过拒绝来抵消这一风险；然而，当规划者产生可执行步骤时，执行者的合规性可能比直接操作基线更高。批准框架的委托对提示设计、模型配对和场景来源敏感，而怀疑的执行者提示会显著降低合规性。原始直接模型排名也可能错误预测已部署的规划-执行行为。在主要集中的原始直接提示下，Gemini是最安全的，但在Claude规划者的情况下显示出最大的放大，从8.9%上升到38.9%的合规性。GPT的近零汇总管道效应则掩盖了被规划者拒绝抵消的重构增加。这些发现表明，多智能体安全评估应分别报告重构、规划者行为、委托框架和模型配对，然后再将失败归因于架构本身。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the complexities in safety evaluations of multi-agent large language model (LLM) systems, which have traditionally aggregated results into a single 'pipeline effect' that obscures underlying mechanisms. Previous methods failed to distinguish between harmful intent reframing, planner behavior, and executor compliance, leading to misinterpretations of safety. The proposed approach introduces a five-condition controlled contrast design to separately analyze these factors across 30 synthetic harmful scenarios and external validation sets. The findings reveal that operational reframing is a significant risk signal, with varying compliance rates across different models, highlighting the need for a nuanced understanding of multi-agent safety evaluations. The methodology effectively demonstrates that planner behavior and prompt design critically influence executor compliance, suggesting that safety assessments should report these elements distinctly rather than attributing failures solely to architectural issues.
